The ~30 other legacy routes are left alone deliberately: changing them all at once changes concurrency for all of them and wants measuring, not a blind edit. |
| D-229 | 2026-09-01 | **`MAX_DIFF_CHARS` raised from 50,000 to 400,000**, env-overridable. | Closes C-07. The old comment said the reason was that nothing downstream chunked a diff; `code_chunker._split_diff` has split per file and per hunk for a while, so the cap was outliving its cause and cutting large PRs to their first quarter. Not removed, because a cap still bounds what one button press can spend — what changed is that the bound now clears essentially every real PR, and the spend behind it is quoted before the review runs. |
| D-230 | 2026-09-01 | **`GET /pulls/{n}/files` is paged at 100, up to 30 pages.** | GitHub defaults to 30 per page and nothing asked for more, so `files_changed` silently stopped at 30. That list is the chunker's "files in this change you were NOT shown" manifest — the thing that stops the model reporting a missing control that lives in a file it never saw — so a short list cost precision, not just display. Capped at 30 pages rather than following `Link` forever: a 3,000-file PR is a scope conversation, not a paging problem. |
| D-231 | 2026-09-01 | **`_manifest` lists one row per file, not per unit.** | A chunk holding twelve hunks of one file produced twelve near-identical rows, burying the part of the header that does work — the "not shown to you" list — under noise. Non-contiguous ranges are kept separate (`lines 1-40, 900-980`), because that tells the model something different from `lines 1-980` about what it may assert. |
| D-01 | 2026-08-07 | Legacy modernization ships as **one new product ID** (`modernization`, `kind=WORKFLOW`, `invoke=None`), not an evolution of `migration-execute`. | Product IDs are persisted in `api_keys.scopes`, `api_key_cost_log.agent_name`, `api_key_agent_llm_config.agent_name` (UNIQUE). `registry.py:20-25` says they were chosen to avoid data migration. `swarm` is the precedent for a workflow product with its own dispatch. |
| D-02 | 2026-08-07 | Modernization v1 is **language-agnostic substrate first**; per-pair specialization (Delphi→.NET first) comes after. | User decision. The substrate is the risky part; analyzers are per-language work that doesn't generalize. |
| D-03 | 2026-08-07 | Durable execution = **claim-and-lease on a table row**. No Celery / RQ / Redis / Temporal. | ~300 lines, zero new infrastructure. The repo has no ops story for a broker. |
| D-04 | 2026-08-07 | Dependency graphs come from **deterministic parsers**, never from the LLM. LLM classification is allowed only as a fallback, and must be recorded in `classification_source`. | An LLM graph is silently wrong in a way that corrupts conversion *order*, which corrupts every later file. Invisible until file 300. |
| D-05 | 2026-08-07 | **No compiling or subprocess execution on the API host.** v1 validates structurally in-process; real builds go to the customer's CI via a branch + PR (`autofix_pr.py:90-110` is the template). | Building LLM-generated code from customer source is arbitrary code execution. Also: real builds need the real SDK and package restore, which don't belong on a FastAPI host. **This has been misread once:** it does not cover *parsing* customer source in-process. `analyzers/php.py` links tree-sitter and runs it in the API process, which is neither compilation nor a subprocess, and reading the rule as forbidding that is what kept a real parser off the roadmap for a session. |
| D-06 | 2026-08-07 | Prompt-cache breakpoint sits on the **system block only** (`llm_gateway._cacheable_system`). Tools render before system, so one breakpoint covers both. | The per-request artifact stays in `messages`, after the breakpoint, so it can never invalidate the prefix. |
| D-07 | 2026-08-07 | `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` **deliberately not enabled** despite WAL + `busy_timeout` being turned on. | `reports`/`approvals` FK to `reviews(id)`; there is no test suite and possibly orphan rows, so enforcement could start raising `IntegrityError` in live paths. Enable only alongside an orphan audit. |
| D-08 | 2026-08-07 | Code Review guards belong **inside `CodeReviewAgent.review_code`**, not in `orchestrator.review_code`. | Four of five call paths bypass the orchestrator — see C-08. The agent method is the only common seam. |
| D-09 | 2026-08-07 | **Do not introduce pytest.** New tests follow `scripts/smoke_products.py`: plain script, `check()`/`section()`, exit 0/1, `TestClient`, `llm_gateway.call_tool` monkeypatched. | Matches the repo's existing (deliberate) no-test-framework convention. 163 checks already run this way. |
| D-10 | 2026-08-07 | The deterministic gate is a **floor, not a replacement**: the result is the stricter of (arithmetic over findings, model's own gate). `finding_gate.reconcile_gate`. | Makes the change incapable of weakening any gate issued today — important because these block releases, and `full_review`'s aggregate gate is computed from them. The model can see context a severity tally can't. `model_gate_status` is preserved so the disagreement rate is measurable. |
| D-11 | 2026-08-07 | `risk_score` is **floored to match the gate** (Red≥70, Amber≥40), not recomputed. | A gate and score that disagree ("Red, risk 5") is the signature of a partial response. Recomputing it outright would change `calculate_overall_risk` for every `full` review, for less benefit than fixing the incoherence. |
| D-12 | 2026-08-07 | The four workflows that bypass the orchestrator were **not** re-routed through `orchestrator.review_code`, despite the original plan. | `review_pr.py`/`autofix_pr.py` write their own `save_review` with PR-specific `project_name`; routing them through the orchestrator would double-persist and change that semantics. Putting the guards in the agent method (D-08) already reaches all five paths, which was the actual goal. |
| D-13 | 2026-08-07 | Untrusted-content wrapping in `_resolve` defaults **on**. Transforming endpoints pass `untrusted_mode="transform"`, which wraps with different wording, rather than opting out. | Default-secure. The transform wording tells the model the markers are packaging to drop; skipping the wrapper entirely left the sharpest path open — planted text in a fetched source file steering generated code a customer may commit. |
| D-14 | 2026-08-07 | Chunks are packed using the **character estimate**, not `llm_gateway.count_tokens`. | Packing needs a number per candidate split; an API round trip each would cost more than it saves. `count_tokens` stays available for callers that need precision. Being ~10% off changes packing efficiency, never correctness. |
| D-15 | 2026-08-07 | Evidence is verified against the **whole original artifact**, and line correction is **off for diffs**. | A chunk is a substring of the artifact, so presence still holds and the recovered position is absolute. In a diff the model's `line_start` comes from the `@@` header and means a line in the *file*; a position recovered from diff text means a line in the *diff*. "Correcting" one to the other replaces a right answer with a wrong one. |
| D-16 | 2026-08-07 | Pre-existing findings **still count toward the gate**; only explicitly waived ones are excluded. | Excluding everything seen before would let a codebase with a hundred existing Criticals report Green — worse than a gate that takes work to turn green. They are labelled `is_new: false` so callers can still say "3 new, 41 pre-existing". |
| D-17 | 2026-08-07 | Failed calls are now metered with **`billable=0`** and `status="failed"`, not charged. | Resolves C-04 without a pricing change. A truncation fires after the provider generated (and billed us for) thousands of tokens; those were recorded nowhere, so repeated failures could overrun a cap invisibly. Making them *billable* is still a pricing decision. |
| D-18 | 2026-08-07 | Budget reservations size from `RESERVATION_MAX_TOKENS` (32000) × expected calls per product, not `config.MAX_TOKENS` (16000). | Resolves C-03. Architecture asks for the top tier and `full` is 7 calls under one guard, so the old reservation bounded roughly a fourteenth of what a `full` run could spend. The constant must move whenever any agent's budget goes above it — `unit_converter.CONVERSION_MAX_TOKENS` and `base_agent`'s architecture call both sit exactly at it. |
| D-19 | 2026-08-07 | The modernization schema (6 tables) lives in `services/modernization/store.py`, called from `database.init_db()` via a lazy import, rather than in `database.py`. | `database.py` is 2200 lines and read by everything; these tables are read by one subsystem. Tables are still created in exactly one place at startup, so the convention that matters is intact. |
| D-20 | 2026-08-07 | Modernization meters **per converted file** — one `guarded_call` per unit — which is the opposite of the workflow rule in `agent_guard`'s docstring. | A run is not one request: it is hundreds, over hours, pausable. Per-unit gives real per-file cost, budget accrual as it goes, and a natural stop at the cap. One guard around the whole run would only discover an overspend at the end. `_EXPECTED_CALLS_BY_PRODUCT["modernization"]=1` because each guard really is one call. |
| D-21 | 2026-08-07 | Cycles are broken **deterministically** (intra-SCC edges dropped in `graph.build`) and recorded as a **non-blocking** checkpoint, not a blocking one. | Nothing in an SCC could ever become `ready` otherwise — the run would deadlock rather than convert. Blocking on each cycle would stall every legacy codebase at file zero, since legacy code is full of them. The blocking `plan_approval` gate already puts them in front of a human before any spend. |
| D-22 | 2026-08-07 | `repo_ingest.safe_relpath` rejects **any** `..` segment, including ones that normalize back inside the root. | "Resolves safely" is a much weaker guarantee than "isn't there", and a source archive has no legitimate reason to contain one. Output paths are derived from input paths, so this is a real traversal primitive rather than a theoretical one. |
| D-23 | 2026-08-07 | An unresolved symbol is a **warn**, not a **fail**; a reintroduced secret is a **terminal** failure with no retry. | A legitimate forward reference inside a converted cycle is indistinguishable from a real unresolved symbol, so failing those makes cyclic codebases unconvertible. A secret is a behaviour, not a transient — retrying spends the same tokens for the same answer, and `autofix_pr` already refuses to commit one. |
| D-24 | 2026-08-07 | `database.save_review` now stamps `finding["id"]` onto each finding **before** serializing `result_json`. | Resolves C-11. Findings got a row id at save time but it never travelled back, so a client could see a finding and had nothing to name in `PATCH /api/v1/findings/{id}` — which is why waiving was API-only. Additive; `result_json` gains a key. |
| D-25 | 2026-08-07 | Chunk-count-aware reservations use a **contextvar** (`agent_guard.expect_calls`), sized from `code_chunker.estimate_chunks` at the route. | Resolves C-17. The real chunk count is only known deep inside the agent, after splitting, while the guard is taken at the route — threading it back up would mean every caller of every chunkable agent learning about reservations. Division-based estimate, not a real split: being a chunk out changes when a marginal caller sees a 402, never whether the review is correct. |
| D-26 | 2026-08-10 | `full` gains three **opt-in stages** (STRIDE / VAPT / sensitive-data) rather than splitting into a second `full-plus` product. Title becomes "Core SDLC Review"; **the id stays `full`**. | A second id means new `plan_products` rows, updating every existing plan or customers silently lose access, and two `reviews.review_type` values splitting history — `registry.py:20-25`'s reason for never re-iding. `Product.title` is display-only, so the honest name costs a string and no migration. Same shape as D-01. |
| D-27 | 2026-08-10 | The opt-in stages gate on **flag AND artifact** (`full_review.plan_steps`), and `run()` dispatches off the computed plan (`will_run`) rather than re-deriving each condition. | The plan drives the progress bar, the job's `total_steps` and the budget reservation; when `run()` re-derived the same conditions independently, the two could disagree. Asking for VAPT with no code is a no-op rather than an error — the UI warns, the API stays permissive. |
| D-28 | 2026-08-10 | `full` reserves budget via `agent_guard.expect_calls(count_steps(plan_steps(...)))` at both routes, not the fixed `_EXPECTED_CALLS_BY_PRODUCT["full"] = 7`. | With toggles the real count is 5-10 and only the plan knows which. Reuses D-25's contextvar rather than inventing a second mechanism. |
| D-29 | 2026-08-10 | `full`'s result and persisted review carry **`steps_not_run`**, and the UI renders those as struck-through "not run" pills. | A Green over five of eight possible checks renders identically to a Green over all eight. The gate was already only as broad as the reviews behind it; this is the first time a reader can see how broad that was. Persisted, not just returned, so the omission survives into history. |
| D-30 | 2026-08-10 | `shared.governance_review` returns findings **flattened onto `findings`** and both callers copy it, plus `gate_status`/`risk_score`, to the top level of the result they persist. | They lived only under `result["governance_review"]`, and `load_findings_from_reviews` reads `result["findings"]` while `save_review` derives its counts from the same key — so a scaffold whose auto-review found Criticals reached the release gate as zero findings and showed Green on the dashboard. `strictest_gate` combines the two agents' gates with the deterministic floor, per D-10. |
| D-32 | 2026-08-10 | `:root` sets **`color-scheme: dark`** and `select option` gets explicit opaque colours (`styles.css:22,890`); no `<select>` may use a translucent `background`. | A `<select>`'s dropdown popup is a native widget — it does not inherit a translucent background, so it painted light while options inherited `--text` (`#e2e8f0`): light-on-white, unreadable. `.sidebar-project select` hit this via `var(--bg-input, rgba(255,255,255,.04))`, and **`--bg-input` is not defined anywhere** — the fallback was always what applied. |
| D-31 | 2026-08-10 | `reports` gains **`project_id`**, stamped by `save_report` from the request context, and `GET /api/reports` lists them. | Reports carried only a free-text `project_name`, and `get_report` scoped through `reports.review_id` — which both writers set to `None` (`full_review.py`, `standalone_report.py`), so **every report was a 404 for anyone but a super admin**. Mirrors `reviews.project_id` exactly; the review fallback stays for rows written before the column. |
| D-34 | 2026-08-11 | An admin-selected **platform LLM connection** is a new precedence rung, resolved inside `llm_gateway.call_tool` via `platform_default()` / `resolve_override()` — not pushed through `with_override()`. | `agent_guard.guarded_call` wraps every call in `with_override(override)`, which for a UI caller sets the contextvar to **None** and would shadow any outer value. Resolving at call time is also what makes one setting reach every surface (UI review forms, Agent Hub, Swarm, workflows, jobs, v1) instead of needing a change per route. Precedence: per-request > per-agent key > key global > platform connection > `.env`. |
| D-35 | 2026-08-11 | The active connection's id lives in **`platform_settings`** (`active_llm_connection_id`), not an `is_active` column on `llm_connections`. | The KV table and its `get/set_platform_setting` helpers already exist and already carry a platform-wide toggle (cost optimization). A column would need a partial unique index to enforce "exactly one" and a two-statement update to move it; a single KV write is atomic by construction. `delete_llm_connection` clears the key so a deleted connection can't stay selected as a dangling id. |
| D-36 | 2026-08-11 | `agent_guard` labels the ledger from the **resolved** LLM (`effective_llm`) but decides `billable` from the **caller's** override only. | They answer different questions. "Which model served this?" must follow the platform connection or every UI call is recorded as Claude when it wasn't. "Whose account paid?" must not: a platform connection is our endpoint and our bill, so it stays billable — folding it in would silently stop project budgets accruing the moment an admin selected one, with nothing in the logs to explain it. |
| D-37 | 2026-08-11 | `call_tool` resolves `max_tokens` once for both providers, and a connection's configured `max_tokens` is a **ceiling that clamps** the agent's request, not a default it overrides (`llm_gateway.py:406-424`). | The two paths disagreed: Anthropic used `config.MAX_TOKENS`, OpenAI a hardcoded 4096, so an agent naming no budget got half the room on OmniRoute and surfaced `LLMTruncatedResponseError` rather than a shorter answer. The clamp (added with D-38) exists because agents now ask for 32000, above what several OpenAI-compatible models accept as an output cap at all (gpt-4o: 16384) — unclamped that is a non-retryable provider 400, clamped it is at worst a truncation that says so. Clamping is logged. Only affects requests *above* a configured ceiling; every other case resolves exactly as before. |
| D-38 | 2026-08-11 | Output budgets roughly **doubled across every agent** (floor `config.MAX_TOKENS` 8000→16000, top tier 16000→32000, VAPT/security 14000→28000, GxP generate 12000/14000→24000/28000, governance prose 4096→8000), and `LLM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` 360→900 in the same change. | Truncation is the expensive failure here: the response is discarded, the tokens are still billed (D-17), and the budget is what caused it. Headroom above the longest legitimate response costs nothing — the provider bills tokens generated, not tokens allowed. The timeout is not separable: 32000 tokens at the 50-80 tok/s a Sonnet-class model sustains is 400-640s, so leaving it at 360 would have converted the new budgets into timeouts plus two retries. Still far below the model's real 128000 ceiling. |
| D-33 | 2026-08-10 | The dashboard's "Recent Reviews" table pages off **`GET /api/reviews?limit=30&offset=N`** (`app.js:loadRecentReviews`), not the `recent_reviews` that `/api/dashboard` still embeds. | `get_dashboard_stats` hardcodes `LIMIT 5` (`database.py:684`) and its shape is a published response model (`schemas.py:125`), so raising it would change an API contract to serve a UI need. `/api/reviews` was already paged; it only lacked a `total`, now added via `count_reviews`, scoped through the same `_review_scope_sql` so the count can't disagree with the page. `recent_reviews` is left in place for API consumers and is now unused by the SPA. |
| D-39 | 2026-08-11 | The CareBrief demo — `demo/`, `scripts/seed_demo.py`, `scripts/demo_capture.py`, `demo_contact_sheet.py`, `demo_artifact.py` — lives on the **`demo/carebrief`** branch only, never on a feature branch. `demo/screenshots/` is gitignored everywhere. | Demo material and product changes have different review audiences and lifetimes; interleaving them made a feature branch unreviewable and would have carried 5.3MB of regenerable PNGs (3MB of it one generated HTML page) into history permanently. The scripts are the durable artifact, the pixels are not. Tag `pre-demo-split` marks the pre-separation state. |
| D-40 | 2026-08-12 | The legacy `/api/release-gate` now goes through `orchestrator.run_release_gate` → `workflows/release_gate.run` instead of calling `orchestrator.gate_agent.evaluate_gate` inline. `run()` gained a `findings=` parameter to keep the pasted-findings input shape. | The inline version persisted **nothing**, so the Release Gate page — the platform's most consequential output — produced a verdict that never reached History, could never be picked by the Governance Report's review picker, and never entered the approval queue. `/api/v1/agents/release-gate` for the same product always persisted: one product, two behaviours depending on the door. Not a D-12 case — D-12 kept bypasses that write their *own* review rows; this one wrote none. |
| D-41 | 2026-08-12 | `full`'s stages now receive a **digest of earlier stages' findings** (`full_review._stage_context`, capped at 12, highest severity first) rather than the caller's raw context alone. | Every stage got the same string and its own artifact, so no stage ever saw another's output and the eight agents met for the first time at the gate — a batch of independent reviews wearing one name. A digest rather than the findings themselves, so a later stage still reviews its own artifact. Verified against the live API: the Code Review stage's summary now reads "All findings directly confirm the earlier-stage requirements review…". Cache-safe — context rides in the user message, after the D-06 breakpoint. |
| D-42 | 2026-08-12 | Read-scoping lives in **`backend/scope.py`**, not `main.py`. `visible_project_ids(reader, request)` moved there verbatim; `visible_from_caller(caller)` added for routers holding a `v1_agents.get_caller` dict. | The rule was reachable only by routes defined in `main.py`, so every other router either reinvented it (`modernization._visible_project_ids`) or skipped it — `routes/approvals.py` listed every approval in the deployment to anyone holding a token. Sits beside `context.py` for the same reason: the routers import it and `main` imports the routers. |
| D-43 | 2026-08-12 | A swarm whose **required agent errored** returns AMBER, never GREEN (`swarm_service._aggregate`). | An errored agent is excluded from `scores` and from `agents_failed`, so `required_failed` stayed empty and the swarm could certify clean over a mandatory check that never ran. AMBER rather than BLOCKED keeps the documented promise that "one agent crashing never blocks the gate" — what it may no longer do is come back clean. |
| D-44 | 2026-08-12 | `findings` gained a read side (`GET /api/v1/findings`, `database.list_findings`/`count_findings`) and a Findings page in the SPA. `PATCH /api/v1/findings/{id}` now checks project ownership. | The lifecycle had a write verb and no way to reach what it acted on: waiving was possible only from the review-detail modal of the review that produced the finding, so "what is still open in this project" was unanswerable. The PATCH check is not optional once ids are listable — it previously accepted any finding id from any project. |
| D-45 | 2026-08-14 | Modernization stages are **rows in `modernization_units` with a `kind`**, not a second table and not a second execution model. `stages.py` holds the order as data; `runner.convert_run` is a driver over it. | Claim-and-lease, lease recovery, per-unit metering and the one-transaction checkpoint are the most subtle 200 lines in the subsystem and the most heavily smoke-tested. A `modernization_stages` table would mean a second copy of all of it. `parent_unit_id` already existed for this and nothing wrote it. |
| D-46 | 2026-08-14 | **One unit is at most one LLM call.** A stage needing two calls per row becomes two stages. | It is what keeps D-20 literally true and `agent_guard._EXPECTED_CALLS_BY_PRODUCT["modernization"] = 1` correct without touching the guard. Break it and every reservation in the product is wrong by an unknown factor. |
| D-47 | 2026-08-14 | The conversion unit is a **component** (N source files → M target files), not a file. Members go to a new `rolled_up` status, flipped to `converted` by `complete_unit` in the same transaction as the component. | 1:1 is wrong for the common legacy shapes: a PHP page-script is markup + query + logic and must become three files; thirty page-scripts sharing `header.php` must become *one* layout. `rolled_up` is deliberately not `converted` — otherwise "417 of 523 files" would become true the moment the plan was approved. |
| D-48 | 2026-08-14 | **Two spend gates.** `discovery_approval` quotes a bounded assessment; `plan_approval` quotes the conversion from measured input and a provable ceiling. `agent_guard.estimate_cost()` made public. | The old single gate said "Nothing has been spent yet" and carried no number, because pricing a conversion requires understanding the codebase and that costs money. The ceiling is a fact, not a forecast — a provider cannot generate more than `max_tokens`. A duplicated price table is how a quote and an invoice start disagreeing. |
| D-49 | 2026-08-14 | The converter returns `files[].filename` (bare) plus optional `subdir`; the directory comes from the component and is joined in **Python**. | File 1 wrote `Api/Controllers/X.cs` and file 200 wrote `src/Api/Controllers/X.cs` and nothing compared them. A bare name cannot express that disagreement, so the drift stops being possible rather than being something we detect. Repairing a path is right at *design* time (once, pre-spend, printed on a plan a human approves) and wrong at conversion time, where it would be invisible. |
| D-50 | 2026-08-14 | Scaffolding (`.sln`, `.csproj`, `Program.cs`, `appsettings.json`) is **deterministic — zero LLM calls** — and versions are pinned constants. | A model asked for a `.csproj` produces package versions that look right and do not exist. The package set follows from charter decisions already taken, so deriving it is a lookup. The stage still owns a row, for resumability and metering symmetry. |
| D-51 | 2026-08-14 | The comprehension digest, architecture map and language playbook are rendered **once at plan approval** into `modernization_runs.system_addendum` and concatenated verbatim by `system_for()`. | Re-deriving them per unit from a dict `json.loads` rebuilt could produce a different key order and silently cost full price on all 500 calls, with nothing in the logs but `cache_read=0`. Same argument `charter.render` already makes. Capped at 60k chars: a prefix that grows with the codebase is a prefix that is never cached. |
| D-52 | 2026-08-14 | The PHP→.NET playbook lives in **Python constants** (`services/modernization/playbooks/`), scoped per run from the run's own inventory. Not the knowledge base. | X-01 already records that KB content reaches the *user* message, after the cache breakpoint. The KB is also runtime-editable, which would change the system prompt mid-run. Not scoped per module type either: *k* variants means *k* cache entries, and units interleave by topo level so each is likely evicted before its second read. |
| D-53 | 2026-08-14 | `analyzers/php.py` runs tree-sitter when available and a **real** regex analyzer when not — never falling through to `generic`. `provides` carries path spellings as well as names. | Falling through to `generic` restores the zero-edge defect the module exists to remove, so a parse failure must degrade precision (recorded in `source`/`confidence`) rather than availability. PHP resolves includes by *path*, unlike every other analyzer here, so a file offers its full path and every suffix — unmatched spellings cost nothing, a missing one loses an edge. |
| D-54 | 2026-08-14 | `.inc` is **not** registered for PHP; PHP `.inc` files go through the per-run extension override. | `pascal.py` owns `.inc`, `register()` is last-write-wins over an alphabetical import, and taking it would silently break every Delphi run in the deployment. |
| D-55 | 2026-08-14 | Hazards (`eval`, variable variables, dynamic includes, `extract`, `unserialize` on request data, shell-out) are enforced in **three** layers: prompt rule, `// MIGRATION-BLOCKED:` marker, and a Tier-0 check that **fails** output carrying neither the marker nor a `manual_followup` entry. `validation.BLOCKING_HAZARDS` filters inside the check, not at the caller. | A flag nobody enforces is decoration. An `eval` turned into a `switch` over the three visible cases looks converted, passes review, and drops every case that was not visible — and no later stage can detect it. Hazards *with* a correct target equivalent (`mysql_*`, weak hashes) are deliberately excluded: demanding a marker for those would fail honest conversions. |
| D-56 | 2026-08-14 | `insert_units` **derives** `dependents` from `depends_on` when a caller does not supply it. | `_promote_ready` now navigates the reverse index to decide who may have become ready, so it stopped being informational. A caller supplying edges but not their reverse would leave every dependent blocked forever — and the store is the only place that can guarantee the two agree. |
| D-57 | 2026-08-14 | Charter suggestions are **deterministic — no LLM, ever** (`services/modernization/advice/`), derived from `playbooks.aggregate_facts` + `evidence.summarize`. | The charter is set *before* `discovery_approval` (it is `set_charter` that raises it), so a model call here would be the run's first spend with nothing approved and the screen above it promising analysis is free. Also: a suggestion that differs between two runs of the same codebase is a coin flip with a citation. Same argument as D-04 for edges. The smoke suite asserts `call_log` is unchanged across the whole path — that check *is* the decision. |
| D-58 | 2026-08-14 | `charter.required_for(observed)` may **only remove** a field from the required set, never add one. Evidence that a decision matters raises the field's *emphasis* in the UI instead. | `/api/v1/modernization` is public: a caller whose charter validates today must not start getting a 400 because our parsers learned something. The invariant is asserted directly (`required_for(any) ⊆ REQUIRED_KEYS`) rather than left to reviewer discipline. Loosening is safe in the other direction — a batch program with no database should not be made to answer 'Data access'. |
| D-59 | 2026-08-14 | Charter `help` text names **no framework**; concrete examples come from `advice`, keyed by language pair. `dependency_injection` renamed to `composition`, with `LEGACY_KEYS` folding the old key in `canonical()`. | Every help string used to be a .NET example, so a COBOL→Java user read twelve fields written for someone else — and DI is not a stack-neutral concept at all (meaningless for Go, Python, Elixir). The alias is not politeness: `render()` walks `ALL_KEYS`, so without it a charter stored before the rename would silently drop the value a human typed out of the system prompt of an already-paused run. |
| D-60 | 2026-08-14 | New charter fields are **appended**, never inserted, and land optional. | `render()`'s output must be byte-identical across a run for the prompt cache to hit, and it skips empty values — so appending cannot change what a charter predating the new fields renders to. Inserting would. Landing them optional is D-58 applied. |
| D-61 | 2026-08-14 | Stack-pair keying lives once, in `playbooks.normalize_target`/`lookup`, shared by the playbook and advice registries, with `DOTNET_ALIASES` as the spelling set. | Two lookups meant to agree on what '.NET 8 / ASP.NET Core MVC' matches, normalising it in two places, eventually disagree — and the symptom (PHP playbook, generic charter advice) looks like a content gap rather than a keying bug. `.NET 8` normalises to `net8`, which does **not** contain `dotnet`, so the alias set is load-bearing, not decorative. |
| D-62 | 2026-08-15 | **The diagnostics contract is the interface, not the compiler.** One schema (`{file, line, col, severity, code, message}`) and one endpoint, `POST /runs/{id}/build-results`, accepting either structured rows or a raw MSBuild log. GitHub Actions, a developer pasting `dotnet build` output, and a future sandbox worker are three producers of it. | It closes the compile gap with **no new infrastructure** and keeps D-05 intact — nothing is compiled on the API host, results merely arrive. It also makes the sandbox worker an optimisation rather than a prerequisite, which is what let this ship without Docker. Accepting a raw log matters more than it looks: it is the only path that works before a customer has wired any CI at all. |
| D-63 | 2026-08-15 | Rework needs **no new persistence**: a failing unit goes `converted → ready` and the existing claim loop re-converts it. `rework_attempts` bounds it per unit; `MAX_BUILD_ROUNDS` bounds it per run; two consecutive builds with the same fingerprint raise a blocking `build_stuck`. | `complete_unit` already versions artifacts, so a second conversion supersedes the first without destroying it. The fingerprint hashes `(file, code, message)` for **errors only, never line numbers** — a file regenerated one blank line longer shifts every line while being exactly as broken, and a fingerprint that moved with it would never detect a loop that is not converging. |
| D-64 | 2026-08-15 | A build report revives only a **`completed`** run, never `cancelled` or `failed`. | The branch outlives the decision to stop: CI keeps firing on it for as long as it exists. A "cancel" that quietly un-cancels itself days later is the worst kind of control, and the operator would have no way to see why the run restarted. |
| D-65 | 2026-08-15 | `build_results.summarize_for_prompt` output goes into the **user** message of a re-conversion (`unit_converter.convert(build_diagnostics=…)`), never the system block, and is fetched only when `rework_attempts > 0`. | Two separate failures. In the system block it would invalidate the cached prefix (D-06) for every *other* unit — paying full price on all of them to tell one of them something. And omitted entirely, the loop is a **no-op**: the second attempt sends a byte-identical prompt, produces identical output, and trips `build_stuck` on round two. The smoke suite asserts the two prompts differ, because nothing else would have caught that. |
| D-66 | 2026-08-15 | Comprehension probes are **on by default** and priced inside the Gate A quote; `rules.bundle` is a pure function, so the call count on that gate is a fact rather than a forecast. | Quoting only the architect while comprehension ran behind the same gate would be the exact dishonesty the two-gate split (D-48) exists to remove. Determinism is what makes the number quotable at all: the run will make precisely the number of probe calls the gate named. |
| D-67 | 2026-08-15 | A business rule is a **claim**, not evidence: every proposed rule is checked against the file it cites (`rules.verify`, reusing `finding_pipeline.normalize`/`line_of_offset`), the model's line number is **replaced** by where the quote really is, and only `confirmed` rules reach a prompt. Rejects are kept with a reason. | D-04 applies to a rule exactly as to an edge. The model's own line number is a guess and is wrong often enough that trusting it puts citations in front of reviewers that point at nothing. The rejects are kept because the reject list is the only way to judge whether the pass is worth its money — a verifier whose failures are invisible is indistinguishable from one that passes everything. The two helpers were promoted from private in `finding_pipeline` rather than reimplemented: a second whitespace-normalising substring search drifts from the first the moment either is fixed. |
| D-68 | 2026-08-15 | `PhpCompat.cs` is a **seeded scaffold artifact** written from a constant, emitted under a project directory, with its symbols registered. | The playbook already *instructed* every converted file to call `PhpCompat.LooseEquals` and nothing ever emitted the class — so every file that obeyed referenced a type that did not exist. Same argument as D-50 for `.csproj`, but sharper: 200 model-derived implementations of PHP truthiness each review as correct in isolation and disagree with each other in ways no single-file check can see. Under a project directory because an SDK-style `.csproj` globs `**/*.cs` *beneath itself* — at the export root the file is silently never compiled, which is the original defect with extra steps. |
| D-69 | 2026-08-15 | The CI callback authenticates with an **API key** (`X-API-Key`), not a bearer JWT, and `commit_tree` pushes one commit via the Git Data API, batched at 500 tree entries. | `get_caller` accepts either, but a JWT expires: stored as a repository secret it works until it doesn't, and a build callback that silently stops arriving looks exactly like a build that stopped failing. On `commit_tree`: `commit_file` is one GET + one PUT per file, so 500 files is 1,000 sequential calls and 500 commits. |

| D-70 | 2026-08-15 | An **empty build log is refused**, not read as a clean build. `{"diagnostics": []}` is the only way to claim a clean build. Reading that as "passed" recorded a green round, wiped the `build_fingerprint` the stuck guard compares, and let an operator resume into a rework pass whose prompts were byte-identical to the ones that already failed — a no-op the loop cannot detect, because the evidence that it was one had just been erased. |
| D-71 | 2026-08-15 | The stuck guard also requires **no pending rework** (`store.pending_unit_count(run_id) == 0`). Without this, the blocking checkpoint fired on a loop that had not run once, and reopening would have charged a second rework attempt against units not converted since the first. |
| D-72 | 2026-08-15 | `reopen_units` **defers** a unit that is `in_progress` rather than reopening it, and both its statements are scoped by `run_id`. | Clearing the lease does not stop the worker holding it: it finishes, and `complete_unit` writes the *pre-rework* output as the new current artifact and sets the unit back to `converted`. The reopen is silently lost while `rework_attempts` has still been spent — three of those retire a unit as `exhausted` with no rework having run, and the next build reports a `build_stuck` that is really a race. Reachable on the normal round-2 shape, where CI fires on the same push the run just produced. |
| D-73 | 2026-08-15 | `finding_pipeline.line_of_offset` reconstructs the normalized text as non-empty lines joined by one space, rather than adding `len(normalize(line)) + 1` per line. | A blank line contributes nothing to `normalize(whole_text)` — its newlines are absorbed into the surrounding `\s+` run — but the `+ 1` still advanced the accumulator, so every blank line above a match dragged the reported citation one line earlier. | D-74 | 2026-08-15 | Portrait fields render through one `_bullets` helper that handles strings **and objects**, in the dict's own key order. | Half of `SYNTHESIS_TOOL_SCHEMA`'s arrays are arrays of objects (`actors`, `cross_cutting_concerns`, `contradictions`). A str-only renderer dropped every one of them while still emitting the `###` heading — so the most expensive call in the pass was paid for, stored, and discarded, and empty headings were frozen into the cached system block for the life of the run. The contradictions checkpoint had the same bug: the count reached the human and the body was blank. |
| D-75 | 2026-08-15 | `global.json` uses `rollForward: **latestMajor**`, not `latestFeature`. | The stricter-looking value was the defect: `latestFeature` rolls forward only *within* 8.0.x, so any machine without an 8.0 SDK band failed at **SDK resolution** — "A compatible .NET SDK was not found", before one line of generated C# was read. Reproduced both ways against SDK 9.0.314: `latestFeature` cannot build the scaffold at all, `latestMajor` builds it clean. Every export this product had ever produced was unbuildable for that reason alone, and it read as "the conversion is broken". |
| D-76 | 2026-08-15 | `modernization_symbols` carries a **`namespace`** column, written from the emitting unit's `target_namespace` and overridable per symbol; `symbols_for_paths` selects it and the conversion prompt renders it on each dependency heading. | The converter was told a dependency's name and signature and never where it lived, so it could not write a correct `using` even in principle — and nothing downstream could compute one for it either. A symbol may override because the deterministic scaffold knows exactly where it put `PhpCompat`, while the scaffold *unit* has no `target_namespace` to inherit. |
| D-77 | 2026-08-15 | Missing `using` directives are computed and inserted **deterministically** (`services/modernization/usings.py`), from the emitted text rather than from the model's `references[]`, after placement and before validation. **A using is never removed.** | Same argument as D-50 for the `.csproj`: the answer is already known, so spending tokens to have it re-derived gets a fact wrong. The text scan beats `references[]` because that list is optional and the schema tells the model to omit framework types as `external`; the text is what the compiler sees. Never removing is the asymmetry: an unused using is a *warning*, a missing one is an *error*, and proving one unused needs a C# parser this repo does not have. | D-78 | 2026-08-15 | A **`wireup`** stage runs after `convert` (zero LLM calls) and corrects `.csproj` package and project references from what the converted code actually contains. | The scaffold has to write project files *before* conversion, so its packages came from seven regexes over charter free text and applied to the **entry project only**, and its `ProjectReference` edges came from the architect's prediction. After conversion both are observable. `wireup` was already a declared `UNIT_KIND` that nothing wrote. |
| D-79 | 2026-08-15 | `scripts/build_run.py` builds a run's export with the local SDK and POSTs the log back; `--fixture` compiles a generated scaffold offline and is part of the verification story. | D-05 forbids compiling **on the API host**; a developer-run script is the same tier as the customer's CI, which is the path D-05 named. It fails when the SDK pin regresses, which is how that was confirmed. |
| D-80 | 2026-08-18 | The PHP analyzer recognises **PDO** as data access: the method-call forms (`->prepare`/`->fetchAll`/`->exec`/…), `new PDO`, and — name-independently — **any `->method('SELECT …')` whose first argument is a statement**. | `_DB_FUNCTIONS` held only procedural names, so a codebase reaching its database exclusively through PDO reported `db_apis: []`. Not a display gap: `playbooks.select` keys the data-access mapping table on `db_apis`, so the corpus section explaining how to translate a query was **withheld from the prompt** on every PDO conversion, `db_sites` gave the converter no SQL for a CRUD app, and `advice._engine` had nothing to name the database with. The SQL-literal rule exists because a layered app wraps PDO in its own `select()`/`selectOne()`, which a fixed method list can never see. |
| D-81 | 2026-08-18 | Shell-escape detection requires a **free-function** call — `(?<![>:$\w])\b(exec|system|…)` — in `evidence.py` and `analyzers/php.py` alike, and code-hazard patterns do not run on data extensions. | `\b(exec…)` matched `->exec(`, because `>` is a non-word character. `shell_exec` is in `validation.BLOCKING_HAZARDS`, so `PDO::exec()` — how every PDO codebase runs DDL and pragmas — meant the connection wrapper *every repository depends on* had to emit a `MIGRATION-BLOCKED` marker or fail Tier 0. A faithful EF Core translation of it failed validation, retried twice against a byte-identical prompt (D-89 fixes that half), and the data layer produced no output. `"…management system (sample app for migration testing)"` in a composer.json matched `system\s*\(` for the same reason. |
| D-82 | 2026-08-18 | `_sql_near` finds string literals with a **scanner** (`_string_literals`), not a regex pairing quotes with a six-character minimum. | That regex paired quotes left-to-right, so a shorter literal desynchronised the pairing for everything after it. In `selectOne('SELECT …', ['id' => $id])` the `'id'` cannot close against its own partner, so the scan swallowed the rest of the method and re-opened on a later statement's quote — and **every call site after the first short literal was handed a different statement's SQL at its own correct line number.** Worse than a miss: the converter was actively misinformed. Bound parameters are what produce short literals, so it got worse the more correctly the source was written. |
| D-83 | 2026-08-18 | Route evidence is extracted from **route tables declared in code**, matched on shape (`$router->get('/x', …)`, `Route::post`, `$app->any`) rather than per framework, carrying verb, path template, handler and middleware. | Extraction was gated on the basename being `.htaccess`/`web.config`/`httpd.conf`, so an app whose routing lives in `routes/web.php` reported one route: the catch-all rewrite handing everything to the front controller — the least informative sentence available about a web application. For an MVC target the route table is the most convertible artifact there is, and `url_compatibility` promises URL preservation against it. The handler/middleware window is bounded by the **statement**, not a character count: a fixed window ran into the next route and gave `POST /login` a later route's `['auth']`, and an app that needs authentication to reach its login page is worse than one with no guards named. |
| D-84 | 2026-08-18 | The database **engine** is evidence: the PDO DSN scheme and composer's `ext-pdo_*` each emit a `datastore` row naming it, canonicalised so `advice._PROVIDERS` matches one spelling. | `PDO` is a wrapper, so recording only the API name left `_engine()` returning the literal phrase `"the source's engine"`, which rendered into the binding charter as "EF Core 8 with the matching EF Core provider against the existing **the source's engine** database". The engine was stated twice in plain text and read neither time; the converter was told to pick a provider and never told which. |
| D-85 | 2026-08-18 | An unqualified PHP name resolves through the file's **`use` map** (`_import_map`); the current-namespace spelling is the fallback, not a co-equal guess. `class_constant_access_expression` captures only its **first** child. | `_name_spellings` offered both spellings because "the one that matches nothing costs nothing" — but `graph.resolve_edges` reports every unmatched candidate as an **external dependency**, and that list is what a customer approves at the discovery gate as their third-party surface. A layered app produced ~30 entries like `App\Controllers\EmployeeRepository`, none of them real. The query bug has the same shape: that node has no named fields in this grammar, so an unanchored `(name)` captured the constant as well as the class, making `App\Core\ATTR_ERRMODE` and `App\Core\class` dependencies. `graph._RUNTIME_TYPES` then keeps `PDO`/`RuntimeException` off the list — they are the language, not a third party. |
| D-86 | 2026-08-18 | Inferred references live in **`UnitFacts.soft_requires`**: an edge when they resolve, silent when they do not. The separate field is what makes guessing safe: offering six template roots through `requires` would have recreated D-85's phantom list. |
| D-87 | 2026-08-18 | `set_charter` records `phase="discovery_approval"`, and `_run_view` falls back to the first stage for any phase that is not a stage id (terminal runs keep `None`). | It recorded `plan_approval` while raising the *discovery* gate, so the manifest told the screen the run waited at Gate B while Gate A was open. | D-88 | 2026-08-18 | `_drain` emits a **`unit_started`** event before an LLM unit runs. | Every other event is written after the fact, so an LLM stage produced no event at all while running: a 45-file run sat at `comprehending`, 0%, $0.00, last event `run_started`, for the whole stage — indistinguishable from a wedged provider, and with `LLM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=900` plus two retries the indistinguishable period is up to 45 minutes per call (C-68). `progress_pct` counts source files, so it cannot move during comprehension or design either. |
| D-89 | 2026-08-18 | A **Tier-0 validation failure is fed back** to the next attempt: the failing issues are stored on the unit and rendered by `validation.summarize_for_prompt`, alongside the drift block. | Build diagnostics reach the next attempt and behaviour drift reaches the next attempt; structural validation — the check that fires *first*, and the only one whose verdict is `fail` rather than a warning — did not. So a rejected unit re-sent a byte-identical prompt, produced byte-identical output and spent all three attempts arguing with itself, and the reason a human finally read described the third identical attempt rather than anything they could act on. `prior_findings` was already a `convert()` parameter, so this was wiring rather than design. | D-90 | 2026-08-18 | Component **namespaces are no longer made unique**; several components may contribute types to one namespace, and the sharing is reported as a correction. `output_dir` stays unique. | Invariant 3 was itself the defect. Sharing a namespace is what a namespace is *for*; the thing that genuinely conflicts is a duplicate **type**, which `reconcile` reports and `symbol_conflict` already warns about mid-run. Directory uniqueness has to stay: artifacts are versioned by path, so two components writing one filename lose a file (D-91). |
| D-91 | 2026-08-18 | `complete_unit` raises **`artifact_overwritten`** when a path's previous version belongs to a *different* unit. | Versioning by path is right for the same unit converting again, and silently wrong across units: the loser stops being `is_current`, so it leaves the export and reconciliation with no row anywhere saying so — the same class as two units emitting one symbol, which has warned since it was found. `architecture` keeps `output_dir` unique to make it impossible, so reaching this branch means that guarantee was defeated (a converter returning its own path, or components sharing a directory) and that is exactly when someone needs to be told. |
| D-92 | 2026-08-18 | `sql_string_building` fires on concatenation, or on a variable inside a **double-quoted** literal — never on a variable merely *near* a SQL keyword. | Single-quoted strings do not interpolate in PHP, and a params array is never inside the literal at all. This is reported as an injection the converter is told to fix, so a false positive asks for a rewrite of code that was already correct — and it teaches the reader to distrust the whole list. |
| D-93 | 2026-08-18 | `architecture.normalize_projects` picks the **entry project** by host-sounding name (not `projects[0]`) and makes the project reference graph **acyclic**, cutting one edge per pass and preferring the one pointing at the host. `reconcile._project_reference_cycles` checks the shipped `.csproj` files as a net under `wireup`. | Both halves were the architect's word, and `_csproj` wrote `depends_on` verbatim. | D-94 | 2026-08-18 | The analysis layer checks **itself** before the first gate: `sanity.collect` rolls every extractor's numbers into one persisted `signals` dict and `sanity.evaluate` reports the pairs that contradict each other, non-blocking, on the discovery gate. | D-80…D-93 were one defect fifteen times — a recogniser assuming the fixture's shape — and the contradiction was *already in the database* each time (`datastore: PDO` beside `db_apis: []`; four `CREATE TABLE`s beside zero query sites; fifteen routes beside "1 route"). Writing better recognisers does not fix that; there is always a next framework. `evidence.py` and `analyzers/` are two independent readers of the same source, so disagreement between them is free signal that nobody was reading. | D-95 | 2026-08-18 | Rejected the plan's **`entrypoint_ratio`** (entry points > 20% of files) for **`template_without_renderer`**, and its **`unresolved_import_ratio`** (>30%) for **`unresolved_own_namespace`**. | Measured: the ratio fires on `PHP_FIXTURE`, correctly — in one-page-per-URL code most files really are entry points, so the ratio is right there and wrong on a layered app. What was wrong in the layered case was specific: the entry points *were the views*, and a template is rendered by something by definition. Both replacements are the discriminating signal rather than the correlated one, and both are quiet on every fixture. |
| D-96 | 2026-08-18 | The `hazards` playbook section is deliberately **unmapped** in `sanity._section_contradictions`, and `blocking_hazard_ratio` fires at **10%** of files, not 15%. | Evidence `hazard` rows and the analyzer's `hazard_kinds` are two vocabularies sharing a word — evidence records findings like `sql_string_building`, `hazard_kinds` records constructs that must not be silently translated — so one being empty while the other is not is normal, and `advice.blocking_hazards` already documents that conflating them is a real bug. The 10% is measured, not chosen: the `->exec` false positive took EMS from 2 blocked files to 5 of 45, which 15% would have missed. |
| D-97 | 2026-08-18 | The discovery gate states **denominators**, not findings: how many files were read by a parser, by the pattern fallback, and not at all, beside each count (`sanity.render_counts`, `runner._raise_discovery_gate`). | "1 route" and "we could not read this codebase" were the same sentence on the screen where money is first approved. Zero and unknown are only distinguishable when the population a count was drawn from is next to it — an absence needs a slot before anyone can see it is empty. | D-98 | 2026-08-18 | `architecture.normalize_proposal` **reports** the output-directory dedup it has always performed. | D-90 argued that a silent correction is the problem and made shared namespaces a reported non-correction — but the directory half stayed silent: the loser was renamed to `src/Foo2` and no row anywhere said the architect had put two groups of files in one place. Uniqueness itself has to stay (artifacts are versioned by path, so two components writing one filename lose a file — D-91). |
| D-99 | 2026-08-19 | `wireup.resolve_reference_cycles` keeps the shipped project graph acyclic, cutting **only** an edge no converted file justifies, one per pass, preferring the one pointing at the host. A cycle whose every edge is code-backed is **not** cut — it is reported as a layering problem. | D-93 makes the *proposed* graph acyclic before the scaffold writes a `.csproj`; `wireup` then rewrites the same references from the types the code actually names, and adding those on top of the architect's predictions closed the cycle again. Found by building a real export: `dotnet build` failed during **restore** with MSB4006, before compiling a line, on a run where all 45 files converted and reconciliation correctly reported the cycle. Cutting a code-backed edge is the trap D-93 documents — it turns one build-graph error into a CS0246 per use, further from its cause — so when both directions are real the split between components is what is wrong, and guessing which half to break would hide that. | D-100 | 2026-08-19 | `_wireup_one` takes the **entry project from the design unit's normalized project list** (the same source `_scaffold_one` reads), not from the first component's `target_project`. | Components are ordered topologically, so "the first component's project" is the *bottom* layer on any layered codebase: it resolved to `Acme.Ems.Domain` on a run whose host was `Acme.Ems.Web`. `program_path` then named a file that does not exist, so the deterministic host wiring — which exists precisely so service registrations are not left to the model (a missing one compiles clean and 500s on every request, the one failure a build gate cannot catch) — silently did nothing. Invisible on every fixture, because a single-project fixture makes every answer the same answer. |
| D-101 | 2026-08-19 | The extraction audit is **one call at the end of comprehension**, and a confirmed claim may become an **evidence row and nothing else** — never an edge, never an order. Rows carry `source="model_audit"` and `confidence=0.5`. | D-04 forbids the LLM producing evidence, and this does not breach it: nothing is adopted on the model's authority, only on the authority of a quote `extraction_audit.verify` finds in the customer's own file — the same standard `evidence.py` meets, using the same matcher as `rules.verify` so two verifiers cannot drift apart. End of comprehension because the discovery gate's promise is that nothing has been spent, and because this is still before the portrait is frozen and before the plan gate, so a recovered fact reaches the digest and the human approving the conversion. Every failure path (including a budget refusal) writes an event and leaves the run untouched: a net that can fail the thing it watches is worse than no net. |
| D-102 | 2026-08-19 | "Does it build" is asked as a **non-blocking checkpoint carrying the command**, at the end of reconcile — not run automatically. `build_results.summary_for_run` always returns a state, and `never_built` is one of them. | D-05 forbids compiling on the API host, so "automatically at the end of reconcile" can only mean dispatching to CI, which a zip-uploaded run has no repository for. What the platform *can* do is make sure the question is asked of someone who can answer it. The absence was the actual defect: a run that had never been compiled rendered nothing where the build badge goes, which reads as no problems rather than no evidence — and a 45-file run shipped an export that could not `dotnet restore` with every offline check green (D-99). `build_run.py` now accepts `--user/--password`, because the friction that stopped it being used was an API key nobody had provisioned, and `get_caller` has always taken a bearer token. |
| D-103 | 2026-08-19 | Golden cases are asked for at the **plan gate**, before conversion (`parity.invite`), not only checked for at the end (`parity.gate`). Non-blocking, and the invitation lists the URLs the analysis already found. | A golden case is a recording of the *legacy* application, so a question asked at the end of the run can only ever be answered no — which is exactly what a real 45-file run did, finishing with `parity_not_recorded` in its log having never asked. The plan gate is the last moment before anything is replaced and a human is already standing there. Non-blocking because parity is a customer buying the strongest guarantee available, not a rule the run can enforce. The routes are included because "record the important journeys" is a question and fifteen named URLs is a prompt. |

The durable fix is to stop remembering and start reading: a build reports `NU1102: Unable to find package X with version (>= 8.0.0)` beside `Nearest version: 2.3.12`, and a `Microsoft.AspNetCore.*` package whose newest release is 2.x is definitionally one the shared framework absorbed. Acting on it needs the build's diagnostics threaded into `apply_to_csproj` (which takes none today) and `NU1102` classified as actionable in `_PROJECT_FILE_CODES` (where it is deliberately not, because guessing a *version* restores nothing — removal is a different repair). Open generics, so the type argument never has to be recovered from a signature. Emitted only for what a constructor names — a registration for a service nothing uses is a claim about the application that is not true. |

Both unrestorable packages of the first two live runs arrived as *version 2* of a project file whose version 1 the scaffold had written correctly. The cost is not one bad line: `NU1102` stops the restore, so it carries no file and no line to route to a component, the loop reopens whatever owns the project file, that stage re-emits byte-identical output, and the run trips `build_stuck` — which reads as a conversion failing to converge rather than as a dependency nobody should have been able to invent. The two mistakes cost very different amounts: a miss costs one build round and returns a file and a line routed to the component that wrote it; a false positive costs `MAX_UNIT_ATTEMPTS` conversions of one unit and then fails the component, after which its source files are converted by nobody at all. The old form fired only when a file held no double quotes anywhere, guessing at "did the model get confused" from statistics; that guess rejected three view components whose only single quotes were in `/// <summary>` prose, and it *exempted* any file that used a proper string elsewhere. The exact form is a fact about the language rather than an opinion about the file, so it can be fatal, and it catches strictly more: a substitution sitting beside a correct string used to be invisible. Read from the **converted** side, because the names that break a build are the ones the converter chose; the source dependency graph answers a different question (how many units sit above this one, and so how risky a rewrite is) and cannot answer this one.
